# Module reference

Generated by `node tools/gen-modref.mjs` from the module definitions — do not edit by hand. In the app the same list is built live (help → Module reference).

615 modules. Pin types: see [Wires and port types](wires.md). The manual text is the first paragraph from the [README](../README.md).

## Analysis

### Activity Events

`actlog` · used in 1 preset

- **In:** `spec` (spec), `lo` (num), `step` (num)
- **Out:** `rec` (rec), `active` (num), `count` (num)
- **Parameters:** `lo` — first channel centre, Hz (0 — start of the spectrum + half a step); `step` — channel step, Hz; `count` — channels; `bw` — channel width, Hz (0 — 80 % of the step); `level` — channel level: mean power of the band, or its strongest bin (peak: weak narrow carriers, CW, narrow FM stand out more, a bit more false alarms); `thr` — busy above the channel floor, dB; `hys` — hysteresis, dB; `hold` — hold, ms (a pause shorter than this does not end a transmission); `minDur` — shortest event, ms; `learn` — learn the noise floor first, ms; `avg` — smoothing, ms (averages the noise: a lower threshold is safe); `common` — ignore band-wide level changes; `rise` — floor adaptation, dB/s (follows the drift; the floor is a low quantile of the level, frozen while busy)

### Air Diary

`airdiary` · used in 1 preset

- **In:** `spec` (spec), `pause` (num)
- **Out:** `rows` (bands), `tx` (bands), `bursts` (bands), `rec` (rec), `count` (num), `occ` (num), `active` (num)
- **Parameters:** `diary` — diary name (stored in the browser; empty — only until the page closes); `mapView` — map rows: hours of the day (all days together) or each day; `show` — days on the map and in the history (older is forgotten); `slot` — time slot, min (the diary is kept per slot; the map has hour rows); `res` — cell width, Hz (auto — the bin width of the spectrum on the 1-2-5 grid). A change starts a new diary; `thr` — busy above the floor by, dB (the floor is the median of the frame); `confirm` — frames in a row before a transmission starts; `hold` — a transmission ends after it is gone for, ms; `fps` — frames per second taken from the spectrum (more is not better: time is counted in frames); `minBursts` — transmissions needed to call a transmitter periodic; `regular` — regularity, % of intervals that fit the period (a skipped repeat counts); `tol` — period tolerance, %; `contMin` — a carrier that does not go off for this long, s — continuous; `minDays` — “daily”: the same hour on this many days (and on 60% of the days seen); `fmin` — map from, Hz (0 — all observed); `fmax` — map to, Hz (0 — all observed); `minOcc` — rows output: cells busy at least, %; `on` — recording (off — the diary only shows what it has)

- **Out:** `bands` (bands), `text` (txt), `count` (num), `f` (num), `occ` (num)

### PSK/QAM Receiver (EVM)

`pskRx` · used in 1 preset

- **In:** `in` (iq)
- **Out:** `out` (iq), `evm` (num), `mer` (num), `foff` (num), `lock` (num)
- **Parameters:** `mod` — modulation; `baud` — symbol rate, Bd (≥ 2 samples per symbol at the input); `alpha` — RRC roll-off; `tbw` — timing loop bandwidth, × symbol rate; `cbw` — carrier loop bandwidth, × symbol rate; `win` — EVM window, symbols

### Signal Analyzer

`sigLab` · used in 2 presets

- **In:** `iq` (iq), `in` (sig), `go` (num)
- **Out:** `blk` (blk), `rec` (rec), `bin` (bin), `text` (txt), `baud` (num), `count` (num), `freq` (num), `offset` (num), `bw` (num)
- **Parameters:** `mod` — modulation (auto — from the signal; ASK includes OOK; raw — the input is already demodulated data, e.g. NFM audio of an FSK transmission); `levels` — symbol levels (2 — a bit per symbol, 4 — a dibit); `code` — line code of the bits (auto: Manchester when the pairs show it, else NRZ); `inv` — invert; `sync` — sync word, hex (e.g. 85CFAB45): the table is aligned on it, the preamble stays before it; empty — found by comparing the messages; `cut` — cut a long message into frames at the sync word (no word given: at a word that repeats); `mode` — capture: rolling window, one buffer, or one buffer on a level; `secs` — capture length, s; `diff` — table: dim the columns that are the same in all messages (shows the fields that change); `sgram` — spectrogram above the plot (time → right, frequency ↑): drag up/down on it to pick a band; outputs freq / offset / bw give the band for IQ Frequency Shift and IQ Decimator; `bpOn` — band-pass filter: analyse only the picked band (it is moved to 0 Hz first); `fmt`; `labels`

### Signal Database

`sigDb` · used in 2 presets

- **In:** `bw` (num), `dev` (num), `baud` (num), `freq` (num)

- **Parameters:** `bw` — occupied bandwidth, Hz (0 — unknown); `baud` — symbol rate, Bd (0 — unknown); `dev` — FM deviation, Hz (0 — unknown); `freq` — frequency, Hz (0 — unknown); `top` — candidates shown; `min` — hide candidates below, %

### Signal Detector (CFAR)

`cfar` · used in 5 presets

- **In:** `spec` (spec), `skip` (bands), `fmin` (num), `fmax` (num), `guard` (num), `train` (num), `thr` (num), `minW` (num), `top` (num), `hold` (num), `edge` (num), `plan` (bands), `blCur` (num), `skipSig` (num), `blAdd` (num), `blClear` (num), `favCur` (num), `favClear` (num)
- **Out:** `count` (num), `rec` (rec), `f1` (num), `l1` (num), `snr1` (num), `bw1` (num), `floor` (num), `bands` (bands), `fav` (rec), `bl` (rec), `listenF` (num), `todo` (num), `gate` (num), `f2` (num), `l2` (num), `f3` (num), `l3` (num), `f4` (num), `l4` (num)
- **Parameters:** `auto` — auto range (full source span); `fmin`; `fmax`; `method` — noise estimate (OS robust, SO one-side neighbour, CA mean); `guard` — guard bins; `train` — training bins; `thr` — threshold, dB; `minW` — min width; `edge` — trim edges, % (receiver windows sag at the edges); `bwDb` — occupied bandwidth: dB below peak (not below threshold); `confM` — confirm: hits (M); `confN` — confirm: of last frames (N); `top` — how many to show; `hold` — hold time, ms (a signal that vanished is waited for this long: the scanner stays, the gate is already closed); `grid` — snap to the channel step of the plan ranges (one signal per channel); `adjDb` — adjacent channel: drop a signal this many dB below its neighbour (0 = off); `dwell` — listen to each signal at most, ms (0 = while it lasts)

### Signal Type Identifier

`sigid` · used in 8 presets

- **In:** `spec` (spec), `in` (sig), `plan` (bands), `fmin` (num), `fmax` (num)
- **Out:** `bands` (bands), `type` (txt), `f` (num), `conf` (num), `count` (num), `bw` (num), `tune` (num), `demod` (val), `sr` (num), `branch` (num), `decoder` (txt)
- **Parameters:** `auto` — whole spectrum; `frange` — search range, Hz; `fftSize` — FFT size (audio input); `period` — analysis period, s; `thr` — detection threshold, dB; `maxSig` — signals to label; `iq` — sample analysis (IQ / audio); `rangeCtx` — use the frequency range: a service of the signal database whose band holds the signal names it (and the decoder from the catalog); `deep` — weak signals: deep integration, s (0 — off). Averages the spectrum for that long and looks for signals with matched filters of several widths; `zthr` — weak signals: detection threshold, σ of the smoothed spectrum (lower — more weak signals and false ones); `need` — mark the bandwidth needed to receive the signal on the spectrum (catalog entry or measured width + margin, to the standard channel width)

### Sound Classifier (YAMNet, neural)

`yamnet` · used in 2 presets

- **In:** `in` (sig)
- **Out:** `text` (txt), `label` (txt), `score` (num), `hit` (num), `rec` (rec)
- **Parameters:** `engine` — engine: phone — TFLite in the DSP app (on the GPU where the phone allows; the model, 4 MB, is downloaded once into the app), browser — MediaPipe; auto — the phone in the app, else the browser; `gpu` — phone: GPU delegate (OpenGL ES / OpenCL); off — CPU only. If the GPU refuses the model, the node falls back to the CPU by itself; `period` — classify every, s; `top` — classes to show; `min` — min share; `watch` — watch for (e.g. speech, siren, dog; empty — off); `wthr` — watch threshold

### Spectrum Analyzer

`sa` · used in 136 presets

- **In:** `spec` (spec), `bands` (bands), `edge` (num), `sigs` (bands), `marksTxt` (txt), `marksRec` (rec), `m1` (num), `m2` (num), `m3` (num), `m4` (num), `bLo` (num), `bHi` (num), `floor` (num), `top` (num), `fmin` (num), `fmax` (num), `split` (num), `tol` (num), `log` (num), `grid` (num), `gain` (num), `spec2` (spec), `spec3` (spec), `spec4` (spec)
- **Out:** `f1` (num), `db1` (num), `snr1` (num), `centerFreq` (num), `holdF` (num), `f2` (num), `db2` (num), `snr2` (num), `f3` (num), `db3` (num), `snr3` (num), `f4` (num), `db4` (num), `snr4` (num), `fr1` (num), `fr2` (num), `fr3` (num), `fr4` (num), `dF` (num), `dDb` (num), `meas` (num), `measTxt` (txt)
- **Parameters:** `auto` — auto range (full source span); `show` — show: spectrum and waterfall, only the spectrum, or only the waterfall (drag the boundary between them to the edge); `fill` — fill under the trace: flat, gradient (brightest at the strongest peak, fading to the floor) or none; `frange` — range, Hz; `dbrange` — range, dB; `split` — spectrum split; `log`; `grid`; `layout`; `panes` — Panes on screen; `follow` — Follow scanner; `edge` — Trim edges, %; `holdMarker` — Park on marker; `paneDb`; `trk` — markers: follow the carrier within bw; `ant` — markers: antenna length / harmonic source f/k; `peakHold` — peak hold; `vscale` — vertical scale: range (floor … top) or tinySA style (reference level + dB per division); `markMode` — mark mode: drag on the waterfall to label a part of a signal (Alt+drag works always); `markLabels` — mark labels (comma list; 1, 2, 3 … picks one in the dialog); `tickSec` — time ticks on the waterfall, s; `tr1` — trace 1; `tr2` — trace 2; `tr3` — trace 3; `tr4` — trace 4; `avgN` — average: frames (a true mean first, then a sliding window); `det` — detector (a pixel column that holds several bins; auto = peak); `rbw` — RBW: bins merged (carriers keep the level, noise grows by 10·lg k); `mkTrace` — markers read trace; `unit` — level unit (dBm / dBµV: shown level = dBFS − gain + offset); `lvlOff` — level offset at 0 dB gain, dB (the range sliders stay in dBFS); `slice` — level vs time at the active marker (inset over the waterfall); `sliceSec` — time inset: window, s; `dRef` — delta markers: reference marker (the others show Δf and ΔdB to it); `meas` — measure at the active marker; `measBw` — measure: channel width, Hz (a receiver channel under the marker sets its own); `measPct` — OBW: % of the power; `measX` — x dB bandwidth: dB below the peak; `acpOff` — ACPR: adjacent channel offset, Hz; `wfBright` — waterfall brightness, dB (+ lifts weak signals out of the noise); `wfContrast` — waterfall contrast (× — narrows the colour window); `phosphor`

### Spectrum Baseline

`specBase` · used in 1 preset

- **In:** `spec` (spec)
- **Out:** `bands` (bands), `rec` (rec), `base` (spec), `count` (num), `f` (num), `excess` (num), `learned` (num)
- **Parameters:** `mode` — monitor: report deviations; learn only: just update the norm; `res` — cell width, Hz (auto — the bin width of the spectrum on the 1-2-5 grid); `learn` — visits to learn a cell before it is checked; `adapt` — norm adapts over, visits (a permanent new station is absorbed after ≈ 2× this); `thr` — report above the norm by, dB; `z` — … and by more than this many σ of the cell; `sdMin` — smallest σ of a cell, dB (a quiet cell is not over-sensitive); `confirm` — frames in a row before a report; `hold` — event ends after it is gone for, ms; `wide` — wider than this, kHz — a wideband event (jammer, raised floor); `missing` — report carriers that were almost always present and vanished

`specstat` · used in 1 preset

- **In:** `spec` (spec), `thr` (num), `tau` (num), `floor` (num), `log` (num)
- **Out:** `stat` (spec)

### Speech to Text (Whisper, neural)

`whisper` · used in 2 presets

- **In:** `in` (sig)
- **Out:** `text` (txt), `partial` (txt), `full` (txt), `go` (num), `rec` (rec), `count` (num), `busy` (num)
- **Parameters:** `engine` — engine: app — whisper.cpp on the phone (DSP app: dotprod CPU, GPU via OpenCL where there is one; knows Russian far better), browser — ONNX in the page; auto — the phone engine in the app, else the browser; `appModel` — phone model (medium-q5_0 ≈539 MB good Russian and keeps up on most phones, large-v3-turbo-q5_0 ≈574 best Russian but needs the GPU or a fast phone, small-q5_1 ≈181 faster, weaker; downloaded once into the app); `appGpu` — phone: use the GPU (OpenCL, Adreno 7xx / 8xx); off — CPU only. Switching CPU → GPU needs an app restart; `beam` — phone: beam search width (1 — greedy, fastest; 3–5 more exact, slower); `partial` — phone: live draft — while a phrase is still being spoken its tail is recognised again every few seconds and shown on the «partial» pin (grey in the node); the final text replaces it at the pause. Runs only when no final phrase waits and slows down by itself on a slow phone; `appFastMode` — phone: short encoder window (the cost follows the length of the phrase, not the 30 s window — several times faster on short phrases, a bit less exact): auto — on when the GPU is not used (on the CPU big models are too slow without it), on / off; `prompt` — phone: hint text for the decoder (names, terms, punctuation style: «Здравствуйте. Приём, как слышно?»); `model` — browser engine, model (tiny ≈45 MB fast, base ≈85, small ≈270 more exact, large-v3-turbo ≈800 MB — the most accurate, heavy: needs a fast PC; .en — English only); `lang` — language (auto — detect each phrase; a fixed one is more reliable on radio); `task` — transcribe, or translate to English; `listen` — listen to the input; `thr` — speech threshold, dBFS (the node also follows the noise level); `pause` — pause that ends a phrase, s; `maxlen` — longest phrase, s; `junk` — drop typical Whisper inventions on silence ("Thank you.", [BLANK_AUDIO], loops); `norep` — fight word loops («слово слово слово…»): decoder bans repeated 5-grams, long runs are collapsed; `stamps` — time stamps [m:ss] in the transcript

### Pins (custom)

`pinsx` · used in 1 preset

- **Parameters:** `on` — connect (the other side creates the pins); `transport` — transport: bridge (tools/termux/bridge.mjs or the DSP app: scripts, GNU Radio, other tabs) or WebRTC (wire WebRTC Data → rtc: the other end is a Pins node, no bridge); `url` — bridge: ws://127.0.0.1:8777 on a computer (tools/node/bridge.mjs), ws://phone:8765 on a phone (DSP app) (other device: ws://IP:PORT/?token=…) — or @name from Settings → Devices; `pins` — pins: ">name" — input (graph → out), "<name" — output (in → graph), comma separated. The other side adds its own; `grow` — let the other side create pins (subscribe to a new name, send a new name, {"t":"make"}, a Pins node over WebRTC); `fmt` — IQ and audio on the wire: float32 (exact), int16 (half the bytes, ~−96 dB steps), int8 (a quarter, ~−42 dB: raw 8-bit SDR samples lose nothing). The receiving side detects it; `buf` — IQ buffer, ms: evens out the network (frames come unevenly, the graph takes IQ every engine tick — without a buffer gaps in the sound). Bigger — more delay, fewer dropouts; 0 — hand over as it comes
- The pins depend on the module settings.

- **Parameters:** `on` — send: connect and publish the pins; `transport` — transport: bridge (tools/termux/bridge.mjs or the DSP app: scripts, GNU Radio, other tabs) or WebRTC (wire WebRTC Data → rtc: the other end is a Pins node, no bridge); `url` — bridge: ws://127.0.0.1:8777 on a computer (tools/node/bridge.mjs), ws://phone:8765 on a phone (DSP app) (other device: ws://IP:PORT/?token=…) — or @name from Settings → Devices; `count` — pins; `names` — names, comma separated (out1, out2… by default) — what clients subscribe to; `fmt` — IQ and audio on the wire: float32 (exact), int16 (half the bytes, ~−96 dB steps), int8 (a quarter, ~−42 dB: raw 8-bit SDR samples lose nothing). The receiving side detects it
- The pins depend on the module settings.

- **Parameters:** `mode` — auto: the hint input (decoder id, protocol or catalog kind from Signal ID / Signal Database / the catalog table) or, with no hint, all protocols listen and the first to decode stays; manual: the protocol below; `proto` — protocol (manual); `giveUp` — auto: a hinted decoder that stays silent this long, ms, is considered a wrong hint — all protocols listen again until the hint changes; `maxCh` — several channels: how many signals are followed at once (the sigs input — marks of Signal Type Identifier: each signal gets its own shift, decimation and decoder choice); `chHold` — several channels: drop a channel after its signal is gone for, ms; `passIq` — iq output: pass the IQ of the channel on (a copy costs CPU; it is always on for a routed protocol such as DVB). With several channels — the channel outCh; `outCh` — several channels: which channel gives the iq and video outputs; `audio` — audio and main-thread decoders (SAME, Morse, DTMF, RTTY, APRS, WWV, SELCAL, FT8, JS8, Olivia, TBSK, GSM): the node runs in the main thread instead of the worker. Turned on by itself when a hint or the manual choice names one of them; they listen in the auto search only when the stream frequency (fc) is known; `rlat` — ADS-B: receiver latitude (surface positions and the distance filter); `rlon` — ADS-B: receiver longitude; `hold` — auto: back to the search after silence of, ms

- **Out:** `out` (sig), `err` (num), `vad` (num)
- **Parameters:** `gain`; `slot` — DMR slot; `uv` — unvoiced quality (mbelib uvquality); `vthr` — voice detect threshold (RMS, int16); `keys` — RC4 keys (40-bit hex): key; id=key; tg:N=key; … ; also from the key input

### WWV/WWVH/CHU Decoder

`wwv`

- **In:** `in` (sig)
- **Out:** `bit` (num), `tick` (num), `sync` (num)
- **Parameters:** `station`; `keep` — log lines

## Geo

### Aircraft Map

`planeMap`

- **In:** `trig` (num), `lat` (num), `lon` (num), `id` (txt), `gsTrig` (num), `gsLat` (num), `gsLon` (num), `gsName` (txt)
- **Parameters:** `ttl` — keep, min

### BLE Signatures (Flipper · tags · glasses)

`blesig` · used in 1 preset

- **In:** `rec` (rec)
- **Out:** `rec` (rec), `alert` (rec), `hit` (num), `rows` (bands), `count` (num), `text` (txt)
- **Parameters:** `flipper` — Flipper Zero (name «Flipper …», services 0x3081–0x3083); `tracker` — tags: Apple AirTag / Find My, Samsung SmartTag, Tile, Google Find Hub; `glasses` — smart glasses: Meta / Ray-Ban Meta, Snap Spectacles, Even Realities, Rokid, XREAL…; `ttl` — keep a device in the list for, min after its last packet

### Clinometer

`clinometer` · used in 1 preset

- **In:** `ax` (num), `ay` (num), `az` (num)
- **Out:** `elev` (num), `height` (num), `dist` (num), `eyeh` (num), `line` (txt)
- **Parameters:** `d` — horizontal distance to the object, m (0 — from the eye height and the base sighting); `eye` — eye (phone) height above the ground, m — used when there is no base sighting or no distance; `from` — standing point (name in Site Plan); `to` — sighted point (name in Site Plan); `tau` — smoothing, s; `bias` — angle offset, ° (put the phone on a level table: the offset is its reading); `flip` — flip the sign (iPhone); `state`

### Echo Ranger (chirp)

`echorange` · used in 2 presets

- **In:** `mic` (sig), `trig` (num)
- **Out:** `chirp` (sig), `dist` (num), `db` (num), `snr` (num), `go` (num), `rec` (rec)
- **Parameters:** `f0` — chirp from, Hz; `f1` — chirp to, Hz (the band B gives the resolution c/2B: 8 kHz — 2 cm; 16–20 kHz is almost silent but weak on phones); `dur` — chirp length, ms (longer — more energy, the range does not suffer); `amp` — chirp level (the Sound Card volume multiplies it); `bursts` — chirps averaged per measurement; `period` — pause between chirps, ms (at least the recording window); `rmax` — range, m; `minDist` — nearest reflector counted, m (the chirp’s side lobes sit around the direct sound); `thrDb` — threshold: echoes down to … dB below the direct sound; `pick` — dist is the nearest echo / the strongest echo; `temp` — air temperature, °C (the speed of sound: 0.6 m/s per °C ≈ 0.2 %); `latMs` — longest expected audio delay speaker → microphone, ms (phones: 50–300); `cont` — continuous: keep chirping, dist follows the last chirps (a sonar); `sim` — simulate instead of the microphone: «distance:amplitude, …», e.g. 2.5:0.4, 4.1:0.2 (empty — the real microphone); `simNoise` — simulation noise

### Follow Detector

`follow` · used in 1 preset

- **In:** `rec` (rec), `pos` (rec), `lat` (num), `lon` (num)
- **Out:** `alert` (rec), `hit` (num), `map` (rec), `rows` (bands), `count` (num), `text` (txt)
- **Parameters:** `idf` — device id field(s), first non-empty (a|b); `places` — seen in this many different places; `sep` — a «place» is at least this far from the others, m; `mins` — …over at least, min; `dist` — …and you moved away at least, m

### Geo from Text

`geoText`

- **In:** `text` (txt)
- **Out:** `rec` (rec), `lat` (num), `lon` (num), `count` (num)
- **Parameters:** `all` — all matches (else first); `loc4` — 4-char locators (more false hits); `id` — record id (empty — none)

### Geofence

`geofence` · used in 3 presets

- **In:** `rec` (rec), `lat` (num), `lon` (num), `acc` (num), `zones` (rec)
- **Out:** `events` (rec), `shapes` (rec), `enter` (num), `exit` (num), `in` (num), `dist` (num), `nearest` (txt), `inside` (txt)
- **Parameters:** `zones` — zones: name: circle lat lon radius_m  |  name: poly lat,lon lat,lon …  (or a JSON array; «zones» input adds more from a Table / Map); `hyst` — hysteresis, m (ignore crossings this close to the edge); `dwell` — confirm after, s (0 — immediately); `accMax` — ignore fixes less accurate than, m (0 — don't check); `first` — report a zone already entered on the first fix; `idle` — recompute at least every, s (0 — only on a new position)

### Geotag

`geotag` · used in 1 preset

- **In:** `rec` (rec), `in` (num), `pos` (rec), `lat` (num), `lon` (num), `acc` (num), `alt` (num)
- **Out:** `rec` (rec), `count` (num), `ok` (num), `lat` (num), `lon` (num), `acc` (num), `age` (num), `dist` (num)
- **Parameters:** `field` — number input «in»: field name of the record it makes (rssi, level, temp…); `every` — number input «in»: one record every, s (the number is sampled, not streamed); `consts` — constants added to every record: key=value; … (dev=sdr1; band=433); `timeBy` — position at the time of: the record (field t, ms or s; without it — arrival) · the moment it arrives here; `maxAge` — a fix older than this (s) is not used — no position; `nopos` — no position: drop the record · pass it on without coordinates; `step` — at most one record per this many metres (0 — all); `dt` — … and not more often than every, s (0 — no limit); `override` — overwrite lat / lon the record already has (off — its own coordinates win); `own` — no position wired — use this device’s GPS (the browser asks permission)

### Image on Map

`geoImage` · used in 1 preset

- **In:** `rec` (rec)
- **Out:** `rec` (rec), `rms` (num), `mpp` (num), `n` (num)
- **Parameters:** `file` — image (a drone orthophoto, a screenshot, a photo from above) — saved to Files; `img` — the image in Files (Images/plot.jpg); `names` — known points in the order you click them on the image (their names come from Site Plan on «rec»); `fit` — fit: auto (2 points — rotation and scale; 3 or more — also skew) · similarity (rotation and one scale only, a flat north-up ortho); `alpha` — opacity on the map; `state`

### Map

`geoMap` · used in 56 presets

- **In:** `rec` (rec), `rows` (bands), `links` (bands), `meas` (bands)
- **Out:** `pick` (rec), `sel` (rec), `edit` (rec), `link` (rec), `lat` (num), `lon` (num), `count` (num)
- **Parameters:** `ttl` — keep, min (0 — forever); `tiles`; `tileFallback` — tiles: if the server refuses (403 / 429 / unreachable), take a spare map for 5 minutes (the note is on the map); `overlay` — overlay layer (relief shading, railways, trails…); `ovAlpha` — overlay opacity; `grid`; `iconScale` — icon size ×; `labels`; `circles` — radius circles (accuracy, error): all faint, only the selected object, or none; `cluster`; `errWifi` — hide Wi-Fi estimates with error radius over, m (0 — show all); `errBle` — hide BLE estimates with error radius over, m (0 — show all); `errBt` — hide Bluetooth estimates with error radius over, m (0 — show all); `errCell` — hide cell tower estimates with error radius over, m (0 — show all); `svDots` — survey marks (input meas): of the selected device only, or of all devices passing the filter; `svTrack` — survey: the walked track; `follow`; `net` — online; `mode` — mode: view / edit (select, drag, form) / add (tap = new point) / line, polygon, ruler, circle (tap = vertex); `drawKeep` — stay in the drawing mode after a shape is finished (draw the next one right away; off — back to «edit» each time); `tools` — mode buttons on the map (for tiles without settings); `vfile` — Load vector layer: GeoJSON / KML / GPX; `osmKind` — objects from OpenStreetMap

### Mark Point

`geoMark` · used in 4 presets

- **In:** `lat` (num), `lon` (num), `alt` (num), `rssi` (num), `snr` (num), `azimuth` (num), `elevation` (num), `freq` (num), `go` (num)
- **Out:** `rec` (rec), `count` (num)
- **Parameters:** `rssiM` — level by hand, dBm (used while the rssi input is not wired); `auto` — auto every, s (0 — off); `session` — session: field (moving about) or quiet (a calm place, a long listen for locating); `tx` — source being measured (field «tx»: Source Locator groups by it); `prefix` — name prefix: P1, P2…; `rx_ant` — receiving antenna (model, polarisation); `rx_gain` — antenna gain, dBi; `rx_h` — antenna height above ground, m; `icon`; `note`

### Measure Entry

`measureentry` · used in 3 presets

- **In:** `v` (num), `go` (num)
- **Out:** `lines` (txt), `rec` (rec), `n` (num)
- **Parameters:** `kind` — what is measured; `from` — from point (A); `to` — to point (B; the vertex of an angle); `third` — third point (C) — angles only; `value` — value: a number or an expression like on a calculator (12.4+3.6*2 · sqrt(3^2+4^2) · hypot(3;4) · sin cos tan atan in degrees · pi · ^ — power; function arguments through «;»); `chain` — chain: after A-B the next one is B-C (names go A, B, C … Z, A1, B1 …); `clear` — clear the value after adding; `sigma` — error of this measurement, m or ° (0 — the Site Plan default); `hd` — clinometer: horizontal distance, m (a number or an expression); `eye` — clinometer: eye height, m (0 — the Site Plan default); `log` — the log (editable, one measurement per line)

### My Position

`geoMe` · used in 31 presets

- **In:** `lat` (num), `lon` (num), `acc` (num), `alt` (num), `speed` (num), `heading` (num)
- **Out:** `lat` (num), `lon` (num), `acc` (num), `alt` (num), `speed` (num), `heading` (num), `grid` (txt), `rec` (rec)
- **Parameters:** `src` — source (wire — lat / lon from the inputs, e.g. Phone sensors); `lat` — lat, °; `lon` — lon, °; `grid` — locator (sets lat/lon); `look` — look direction, ° (when GPS has no heading); `fov` — field of view, ° (0 — none); `fovKm` — field of view range, km; `rings` — distance rings, km (e.g. 1,5,10); `maxacc` — GPS: ignore fixes worse than … m (0 — take all; the last good one is kept while waiting); `avg` — GPS: average the fixes of the last … s, weights 1/accuracy² (0 — the latest): the random error falls ≈ 1/√N, a systematic one does not

### Photo Measure

`photomeasure` · used in 3 presets

- **Out:** `lines` (txt), `dist` (num), `len` (num), `area` (num), `n` (num), `rec` (rec)
- **Parameters:** `file` — photo (a phone offers the camera); `ref` — reference in the frame (lying in the same plane as what you measure) · or the 2-point scale of a scan / map / satellite screenshot; `rd` — scan scale: the real distance between the 2 clicked points, m; `up` — scan north: bearing of the image’s «up», ° (0 — up is north; 90 — up is east); `rw` — custom reference width, m (top edge); `rh` — custom reference height, m (side edge); `mode` — lines: ground / floor (distances between points) · wall (width and height; the reference’s top edge is horizontal) · trace (coordinates «P1 at x y» in metres, north up — for a scan / map); `prefix` — point name prefix for Site Plan (P → P1, P2…); `poly` — add «poly» (a contour through the points in order) to the lines; `state`

### Plan Editor

`planedit` · used in 21 presets

- **In:** `rec` (rec), `lat` (num), `lon` (num), `alt` (num), `az` (num), `el` (num), `fov` (num)
- **Out:** `lines` (txt), `rec` (rec), `area` (num), `loss` (num), `vent` (num), `cover` (num), `sig` (num), `ncam` (num), `nant` (num), `margin` (num)
- **Parameters:** `tool` — tool (also the strip on the canvas); `view`; `vaz` — 3D: turn, °; `vel` — 3D: elevation, °; `snap` — snap to the grid, m (0 — off); `ortho` — walls snap to 15° steps; `wsnap` — devices on walls: a socket, switch, panel, load, radiator, boiler, heater, manifold put within 0.6 m of a wall of the floor stands on its face (the side you tapped; inside the wall — the room side); `lv` — level: all (3D — the house above the ground with the roof, plan — the ground floor) · a number — that floor (3D — it and the ones below; 0 — ground, −1 — cellar); the signal is computed 1 m above its floor; `camview` — the view from the selected camera (3D, a window in the corner; tap it to enlarge); `ztgt` — cameras: the target height above the floor, m (a person ≈ 1, a face ≈ 1.6, a car number plate ≈ 0.5) — a wall, a fence or a box hides it only where the line of sight passes within their height; `refl` — reflections: 0 — the direct ray only · 1 — plus one bounce off a wall · 2 — plus two (slower); the powers of the paths add up, each bounce costs by the material (metal 1, concrete 5, glass 6, brick 7, block 8, wood 9, drywall 11 dB); the rays to a receiver are drawn on the «signal» layer; `layer` — layer: cameras (green — seen, red — blind) · signal (antennas, dBm) · climate (rooms: heat loss, W/m², air flow) · loads (bearing walls: kN/m and the share of the capacity; the readout: lintels, slabs, rafters, foundation); `thr` — signal threshold, dBm (below — dim); `cell` — coverage cell, m; `lfreq` — signal layer: only the transmitters on this frequency, MHz (0 — all; ±1 %, at least 10 MHz); `uon` — underlay (Photo Measure rec → here): show; `uop` — underlay opacity; `pkind` — PLAN PLACEMENT — what to place (the template is the selected camera / antenna, else the first one, else the defaults; devices already placed by hand count as covering); `pn` — how many at most (0 — as many as the target needs); `ptarget` — target: % of the rooms seen / above the signal threshold (the threshold is the one in the signal layer); `pmount` — cameras: where they may be mounted; `pdoors` — cameras: doors and windows weigh ×3 (a 1.5 m zone around them matters more); `escale` — EXPORT — SVG scale 1:…; `edims` — SVG: wall dimensions; `ecov` — SVG: camera view zones; `prjname` — project name (the title block of the sheet); `prjaddr` — project address / object; `prjby` — project: drawn by; `paper` — SHEET — paper (the scale is chosen from 1:20 … 1:1000 so that the plan fits; the floor on is the one drawn); `pname` — point name; `wt` — wall thickness, m (new walls take it too); `wh` — wall height, m; `mat` — wall material: signal loss per 10 cm at 2.4 GHz — brick 4, concrete 6, aerated block 3, drywall 1.5, wood 2, glass 1, metal 20 dB; it grows with the frequency as f^k (brick 0.8, concrete 0.9, drywall 0.5, wood 0.6, glass 0.6, metal 0.1); `wlen` — locked length, m (0 — free): the side keeps it while you drag the points; `ow` — width, m; `od` — depth, m (box); `oh` — height, m; `orot` — rotation, °; `osill` — sill / base height, m; `cname` — camera name; `cz` — camera height, m; `caz` — camera azimuth, ° (drag the white handle); `ctilt` — camera tilt down, °; `cfov` — camera field of view (horizontal), °; `crange` — camera range, m; `aname` — antenna name; `az2` — antenna height, m; `aaz` — antenna azimuth, ° (drag the white handle); `abw` — beam width, ° (360 — omni); `arole` — antenna type: transmit (access point, base station) / receive (a phone, a sensor: its level and margin are calculated); `aeirp` — EIRP, dBm (Wi-Fi 20); `again` — receiver gain, dBi; `asens` — receiver sensitivity, dBm (the margin is the level above it); `afreq` — frequency, MHz; `wzw` — house width, m (west–east); `wzd` — house depth, m (south–north); `wzf` — floors (the upper ones copy the lower: bedrooms and a bath, no entrance door); `wzlay` — layout: one room · living + kitchen · + bath · living, bedroom, kitchen, bath; `wzroof` — roof: gable · hip · shed · none (the overhang is 0.5 m); `wzang` — roof slope, ° (snow load falls with it: none at 60°); `rw` — new room width, m (the button below places a room rw × rh); `rh` — new room depth, m; `show` — show: everything · structure only · electrics · heating · ventilation · cameras · radio (the other systems are hidden and not clickable; walls, rooms and doors always stay); `chk` — plan check: mark geometry problems on the plan (a ! in a ring) and list them in the readout; `ldsnow` — loads: snow kPa (SP 20 districts I…VIII; «city» — from the city above, 1.5 if custom); `ldsoil` — loads: soil under the foundation (design resistance R0: gravel 400, sand 300, semi-solid clay 250, loam 200, plastic clay 150, wet silt 100, peat 60 kPa); `lddepth` — loads: foundation depth, m (below the freezing depth for heaving soils); `ldfloor` — loads: the floors — monolithic RC slab (the slab of Site Plan, 0.22 m), hollow-core slab, timber beams; `ldroof` — loads: roof dead load kPa over the slope (rafters, battens, insulation, covering); `ldpile` — loads: bearing capacity of a pile, kN (the piles alternative of the foundation); `hvtype` — climate device (the ♨ tool places it): boiler, radiator, floor (underfloor loop), heater (local: convector, stove, fireplace), manifold, tee (a free branch of a pipe); ahu (supply-extract unit with recovery), fan, supply, extract. The ≋ tool joins them: pipes between water devices, ducts between air ones; `hvcity` — city: sets the outdoor design temperature and the degree-days of the season (approximate; «custom» — the temperature below, 5000 degree-days); `hvtout` — outdoor design °C: the coldest five days of the region (heat loss; ignored when a city is set); `hvwater` — water supply/return °C (70/50 — a condensing boiler; 55/45 — a heat pump; 35/30 — underfloor); `hvins` — wall insulation, mm of EPS (λ 0.04) on the outer walls; `hvglaz` — windows U, W/(m²·K) (heat loss); `scene`

### Plan Studio

`planStudio` · used in 1 preset

- **In:** `rec` (rec), `lat` (num), `lon` (num), `acc` (num), `alt` (num), `lines` (txt), `file` (txt), `az` (num), `el` (num), `fov` (num), `rows` (bands), `links` (bands), `meas` (bands)
- **Out:** `area` (num), `perim` (num), `slope` (num), `rms` (num), `n` (num), `rec` (rec), `poly` (rec), `lines` (txt), `loss` (num), `vent` (num), `room_area` (num), `cover` (num), `sig` (num), `ncam` (num), `nant` (num), `margin` (num), `pick` (rec), `sel` (rec), `edit` (rec), `link` (rec), `pick_lat` (num), `pick_lon` (num), `count` (num), `mpp` (num)
- **Parameters:** `tab` — tab: Plan (measurements, 3D) · Editor (walls, rooms, devices) · Map (tiles, your shapes) · Photo (your own aerial image as a layer); `sp_plan` — measurements, one per line: A-B 12.4 · A-B h 12.4 · A-B dz 1.2 · A-B az 45 · A-B-C 90 · A-B tilt 25 15 eye 1.6 · A z 120 · A gps lat lon ±3 · A at x y · poly A B C D · room Hall 4.48 3.74 4.46 3.74 (corners Hall1…, 90° unless «side/angle», «side/?» — not measured; d — diagonal 1-3) · wall Hall2-Hall3 Kit1-Kit4 0.4 [shift s] (rooms across a wall) · room … h 2.65 / height 2.7 (ceiling) · window zal1-zal2 1.20 1.40 h 1.45 sill 0.85 / door … (from corner, width) · outer 0.45 concrete / inner drywall / material A-B glass (walls) · mirror (flip left ↔ right) (± — error, m or °); `sp_file` — plan file in Files (e.g. Plans/dacha.txt; the pin «file» overrides). The measurements are read from it and your edits are written back; a new name creates the file from the current text. Empty — the first edit of the text creates Plans/siteplan-DATE-TIME.txt by itself; `sp_fsave` — save edits of the measurements to the file automatically; `sp_tpl` — insert a line: pick a word — the line with the names of the first room, its first side and a free device name goes to the end of the text (measurement, room, window, door, use, socket, light, switch, radiator, boiler, floor, heater, tee, manifold, pipe, duct, ahu, supply, extract); `sp_table` — Table lists with measurements, comma-separated. Columns: kind (d h dz az tilt ang poly) + a, b, c + value [+ sigma]; or name + lat, lon [+ acc, alt]; or name + x, y [+ z]; or a ready «line»; `sp_view` — view; `sp_lvl` — level on the plan: 0 — the ground floor, −1 — the cellar, 1 — the first upper floor (the others are dashed); `sp_grid` — grid: north (E / N axes of the plan) · structure (along the first side of the contour, from its first point) · off; `sp_coords` — label points with their grid coordinates; `sp_az` — 3D: turn, °; `sp_el` — 3D: elevation, °; `sp_zex` — 3D: height exaggeration ×; `sp_sd` — default tape error, m (one line: «± 0.1»); `sp_sa` — default compass / angle / clinometer error, °; `sp_sg` — default GPS error, m (phone ≈ 3–5, dual-frequency 1–2, RTK 0.02); `sp_eye` — clinometer: eye height above the point, m (a line: «eye 1.6»); `sp_ground` — GPS points take height from the Horizon terrain (weak, ±«terrain error»); `sp_pname` — name for «＋ GPS fix» (empty — P1, P2…); `pe_abw` — beam width, ° (360 — omni); `pe_arole` — antenna type: transmit (access point, base station) / receive (a phone, a sensor: its level and margin are calculated); `pe_aeirp` — EIRP, dBm (Wi-Fi 20); `pe_again` — receiver gain, dBi; `pe_asens` — receiver sensitivity, dBm (the margin is the level above it); `pe_afreq` — frequency, MHz; `pe_wzw` — house width, m (west–east); `pe_wzd` — house depth, m (south–north); `pe_wzf` — floors (the upper ones copy the lower: bedrooms and a bath, no entrance door); `pe_wzlay` — layout: one room · living + kitchen · + bath · living, bedroom, kitchen, bath; `pe_wzroof` — roof: gable · hip · shed · none (the overhang is 0.5 m); `pe_wzang` — roof slope, ° (snow load falls with it: none at 60°); `pe_rw` — new room width, m (the button below places a room rw × rh); `pe_rh` — new room depth, m; `pe_show` — show: everything · structure only · electrics · heating · ventilation · cameras · radio (the other systems are hidden and not clickable; walls, rooms and doors always stay); `pe_chk` — plan check: mark geometry problems on the plan (a ! in a ring) and list them in the readout; `pe_ldsnow` — loads: snow kPa (SP 20 districts I…VIII; «city» — from the city above, 1.5 if custom); `pe_ldsoil` — loads: soil under the foundation (design resistance R0: gravel 400, sand 300, semi-solid clay 250, loam 200, plastic clay 150, wet silt 100, peat 60 kPa); `pe_lddepth` — loads: foundation depth, m (below the freezing depth for heaving soils); `pe_ldfloor` — loads: the floors — monolithic RC slab (the slab of Site Plan, 0.22 m), hollow-core slab, timber beams; `pe_ldroof` — loads: roof dead load kPa over the slope (rafters, battens, insulation, covering); `pe_ldpile` — loads: bearing capacity of a pile, kN (the piles alternative of the foundation); `pe_hvtype` — climate device (the ♨ tool places it): boiler, radiator, floor (underfloor loop), heater (local: convector, stove, fireplace), manifold, tee (a free branch of a pipe); ahu (supply-extract unit with recovery), fan, supply, extract. The ≋ tool joins them: pipes between water devices, ducts between air ones; `pe_hvcity` — city: sets the outdoor design temperature and the degree-days of the season (approximate; «custom» — the temperature below, 5000 degree-days); `pe_hvtout` — outdoor design °C: the coldest five days of the region (heat loss; ignored when a city is set); `pe_hvwater` — water supply/return °C (70/50 — a condensing boiler; 55/45 — a heat pump; 35/30 — underfloor); `pe_hvins` — wall insulation, mm of EPS (λ 0.04) on the outer walls; `pe_hvglaz` — windows U, W/(m²·K) (heat loss); `pe_scene`; `mp_ttl` — keep, min (0 — forever); `mp_tiles` — tiles; `mp_tileFallback` — tiles: if the server refuses (403 / 429 / unreachable), take a spare map for 5 minutes (the note is on the map); `mp_overlay` — overlay layer (relief shading, railways, trails…); `mp_ovAlpha` — overlay opacity; `mp_grid` — grid; `mp_iconScale` — icon size ×; `mp_labels` — labels; `mp_circles` — radius circles (accuracy, error): all faint, only the selected object, or none; `mp_cluster` — cluster; `mp_errWifi` — hide Wi-Fi estimates with error radius over, m (0 — show all); `mp_errBle` — hide BLE estimates with error radius over, m (0 — show all); `mp_errBt` — hide Bluetooth estimates with error radius over, m (0 — show all); `mp_errCell` — hide cell tower estimates with error radius over, m (0 — show all); `mp_svDots` — survey marks (input meas): of the selected device only, or of all devices passing the filter; `mp_svTrack` — survey: the walked track; `mp_follow` — follow; `mp_net` — online; `mp_mode` — mode: view / edit (select, drag, form) / add (tap = new point) / line, polygon, ruler, circle (tap = vertex); `mp_drawKeep` — stay in the drawing mode after a shape is finished (draw the next one right away; off — back to «edit» each time); `mp_tools` — mode buttons on the map (for tiles without settings); `mp_vfile` — Load vector layer: GeoJSON / KML / GPX; `mp_osmKind` — objects from OpenStreetMap; `im_file` — image (a drone orthophoto, a screenshot, a photo from above) — saved to Files; `im_img` — the image in Files (Images/plot.jpg); `im_names` — known points in the order you click them on the image (their names come from Site Plan on «rec»); `im_fit` — fit: auto (2 points — rotation and scale; 3 or more — also skew) · similarity (rotation and one scale only, a flat north-up ortho); `im_alpha` — opacity on the map; `im_state`

### Point Elevation

`geoElev`

- **In:** `rec` (rec), `lat` (num), `lon` (num), `h` (num)
- **Out:** `rec` (rec), `ground` (num), `alt` (num), `count` (num)
- **Parameters:** `hdef` — height above ground for points without h and alt, m (alt = ground + h); `force` — recalculate alt = ground + h even when alt is set; `zoom` — terrain zoom (10 ≈ 150 m per pixel, 12 ≈ 40 m); `net` — download missing terrain tiles

### Room Walk

`roomwalk` · used in 1 preset

- **In:** `d` (num), `az` (num), `go` (num)
- **Out:** `lines` (txt), `area` (num), `perim` (num), `n` (num), `rec` (rec)
- **Parameters:** `dist` — typed distance to the wall, m (when d is not wired); `azim` — typed azimuth, ° from north (when az is not wired); `rect` — rectangular room: snap the walls to one angle + 90° steps (the compass is noisy, the walls are straight); `merge` — shots within … ° of each other are one wall (averaged); `sd` — distance error, m; `saz` — compass error, ° (phones: 2–5 near steel and wiring up to 15); `prefix` — corner name prefix in Site Plan (R → R1, R2…); `shots` — shots, one per line: azimuth distance (editable; # — comment)

### SDR Receivers Directory

`sdrdir` · used in 1 preset

- **In:** `select` (rec)
- **Out:** `rows` (bands), `server` (txt), `name` (txt), `count` (num)
- **Parameters:** `kind` — receivers; `free` — only with free slots; `q` — filter: name / place / host; `file` — Load list from file

### Site Plan

`siteplan` · used in 24 presets

- **In:** `lat` (num), `lon` (num), `acc` (num), `alt` (num), `rec` (rec), `lines` (txt), `file` (txt)
- **Out:** `area` (num), `perim` (num), `slope` (num), `rms` (num), `n` (num), `rec` (rec), `poly` (rec)
- **Parameters:** `plan` — measurements, one per line: A-B 12.4 · A-B h 12.4 · A-B dz 1.2 · A-B az 45 · A-B-C 90 · A-B tilt 25 15 eye 1.6 · A z 120 · A gps lat lon ±3 · A at x y · poly A B C D · room Hall 4.48 3.74 4.46 3.74 (corners Hall1…, 90° unless «side/angle», «side/?» — not measured; d — diagonal 1-3) · wall Hall2-Hall3 Kit1-Kit4 0.4 [shift s] (rooms across a wall) · room … h 2.65 / height 2.7 (ceiling) · window zal1-zal2 1.20 1.40 h 1.45 sill 0.85 / door … (from corner, width) · outer 0.45 concrete / inner drywall / material A-B glass (walls) · mirror (flip left ↔ right) (± — error, m or °); `file` — plan file in Files (e.g. Plans/dacha.txt; the pin «file» overrides). The measurements are read from it and your edits are written back; a new name creates the file from the current text. Empty — the first edit of the text creates Plans/siteplan-DATE-TIME.txt by itself; `fsave` — save edits of the measurements to the file automatically; `tpl` — insert a line: pick a word — the line with the names of the first room, its first side and a free device name goes to the end of the text (measurement, room, window, door, use, socket, light, switch, radiator, boiler, floor, heater, tee, manifold, pipe, duct, ahu, supply, extract); `table` — Table lists with measurements, comma-separated. Columns: kind (d h dz az tilt ang poly) + a, b, c + value [+ sigma]; or name + lat, lon [+ acc, alt]; or name + x, y [+ z]; or a ready «line»; `view`; `lvl` — level on the plan: 0 — the ground floor, −1 — the cellar, 1 — the first upper floor (the others are dashed); `grid` — grid: north (E / N axes of the plan) · structure (along the first side of the contour, from its first point) · off; `coords` — label points with their grid coordinates; `az` — 3D: turn, °; `el` — 3D: elevation, °; `zex` — 3D: height exaggeration ×; `sd` — default tape error, m (one line: «± 0.1»); `sa` — default compass / angle / clinometer error, °; `sg` — default GPS error, m (phone ≈ 3–5, dual-frequency 1–2, RTK 0.02); `eye` — clinometer: eye height above the point, m (a line: «eye 1.6»); `ground` — GPS points take height from the Horizon terrain (weak, ±«terrain error»); `pname` — name for «＋ GPS fix» (empty — P1, P2…); `save` — Table list for the points

### Source Locator

`geoLocate` · used in 6 presets

- **In:** `rec` (rec), `rows` (bands), `select` (rec)
- **Out:** `rec` (rec), `rays` (rec), `lat` (num), `lon` (num), `alt` (num), `err` (num), `count` (num)
- **Parameters:** `field` — level field; `method` — use: auto — whatever the records have (level, bearing, distance); range — only distances (Wi-Fi RTT, cell Timing Advance); `group` — group by field (bssid…) — one source per value; `labelField` — label field (ssid…); `pathN` — path loss exponent n; `sigmaDb` — level error, dB; `sigmaAz` — bearing error, °; `sigmaDist` — distance error, m (when the record has no distSigma); `useTx` — use the advertised TX power (field txp, BLE) as the level at 1 m: the unknown power is not fitted; `useAcc` — weigh marks by position accuracy (field acc, m): a mark with a poor GPS fix counts less, most of all close to the source; `h0` — assumed source height above ground, m (without elevation angles); `rays` — rays: links from the marks to the estimate (Map, 3D)

### IQ Recorder

`iqRec` · used in 3 presets

- **In:** `in` (iq), `voice` (rec), `rec` (rec), `go` (num), `freq` (num), `tag` (txt)
- **Out:** `sec` (num), `size` (num), `saved` (num), `files` (num), `hits` (num)
- **Parameters:** `mode` — what goes in: everything · voice only (parts of the stream marked by voice records of a decoder: DMR bursts, merged into one file) · go input (while it is above 0.5, with the margin before it rose) · on match (a whole packet whose record from the rec input satisfies «when»); `when` — on match: condition on a record, like the Timeline filter — data~123 · rssi>-70 kind=beacon · word (any column); empty — every record; `plen` — on match: packet length when the record has no dur (ms), counted back from its end; `band` — whole input — the stream as it comes; channel — only one channel (shifted and decimated); `chOff` — channel: offset from the input centre, Hz (the freq input — absolute frequency — overrides it); `chBw` — channel: bandwidth, Hz; `fmt` — format (SigMF keeps voice segments as captures and annotations); `tag` — tags, comma separated (the first goes into the file name); the tag input overrides; `folder` — folder in Files; `autostart` — record as soon as the stream arrives; `padB` — margin before, ms — voice / go / match: taken from the past (the decoder needs the sync word and header before the voice to lock again when the file is played back); `padA` — margin after, ms (for go — it holds the recording after the input falls); `split` — start a new file after, MB (recordings are kept in memory until saved)

### Phone Actions (DSP app)

`phone` · used in 16 presets

- **In:** `go` (num), `text` (txt), `ir` (txt), `torch` (num), `cmd` (rec)
- **Out:** `rec` (rec), `text` (txt), `new` (num), `torch` (num), `ok` (num)
- **Parameters:** `url` — bridge of the DSP app — an address, or @name from Settings → Devices; `action` — action (the Run button, or a rising edge on go); `title` — notification: title; `body` — text of the notification / SMS (the «text» input overrides it); `buttons` — notification: buttons, comma separated (up to 3) → rec {src:notify, action}; `open` — notification: a tap opens the app (no tap event); `vibMs` — vibrate: duration, ms (a single buzz; a pattern or a named effect go through the «cmd» rec input: {cmd:'vibrate',pattern:[…]} or {cmd:'vibrate',effect:'click'}); `vibAmp` — vibrate: amplitude, 0…255 (0 — default; needs amplitude control); `to` — SMS: number; `dataHex` — data SMS: payload, hex (binary SMS to an application port — not a raw PDU; build one with the SMS PDU node); `dport` — data SMS: destination port; `rawPdu` — raw SMS: TPDU hex, no SMSC prefix (the «tpdu» output of SMS PDU, or the «text» input) — experimental, needs a hidden Android API (tools/android/README.md); `rawSmsc` — raw SMS: SMSC bytes, hex (empty — let the network use its default); `box` — SMS list: folder; `q` — SMS list / contacts: search text; `limit` — SMS list / contacts: how many; `level` — torch: brightness level (0 — default; Android 13+); `alarmName` — alarm: name (id; empty — made from the time; for remove empty = all); `alarmTime` — alarm: time HH:MM; `alarmDays` — alarm: days — mon,wed,fri / weekdays / weekend / daily; empty — once; `alarmLabel` — alarm: title on the lock screen; `alarmUrl` — alarm: internet radio stream (http/https, .pls, .m3u, HLS) — plays on the alarm volume; `alarmSample` — alarm: fallback if the stream does not load, drops or there is no network — file path / content:// / address; default — the system alarm tone, beep — built-in beeps; `alarmLoad` — alarm: wait for the stream to start, s (then the fallback); `alarmRamp` — alarm: volume rises over, s (0 — at once); `alarmVol` — alarm: alarm volume, % of the maximum (0 — leave as set); `alarmSnooze` — alarm: snooze, min; `alarmMax` — alarm: stops ringing by itself after, min; `screenFps` — screen recording: frames per second (0 — 30); `screenSide` — screen recording: the long side of the picture, px (0 — 1280; never above the screen); `screenKbps` — screen recording: bitrate, kbit/s (0 — by the size of the picture); `screenAudio` — screen recording: add the microphone sound (the sound of other apps is not recorded); `ussdCode` — USSD: code, *100# style (balance, tariff… — it can cost money; wipe / service codes are refused; 5 a minute at most). The answer of the network comes as a record ussd; `ussdSim` — USSD: SIM slot from 0 (empty — the default SIM; ussd.sims lists them); `dialNumber` — dial: the number (digits, +, *, #); `dialDirect` — dial: call at once (asks for Phone permission; off — the dialer opens with the number and you press call); `mediaKind` — gallery: what to list / read / save (the gallery of the phone, MediaStore; Android asks for access to photos, video or audio once); `mediaQuery` — gallery: list — only names with this text; `mediaDays` — gallery: list — added in the last N days (0 — all); `mediaExif` — gallery: list — the position of the photo from EXIF (lat, lon; asks for «media location»; up to 100 rows); `mediaThumbs` — gallery: list — a thumbnail in the field thumb as a data-URI (a Table card shows it; up to 100 rows); `mediaThumb` — gallery: thumbnail size, px; `mediaId` — gallery: id of the item (from the list) for media.thumb / media.read; `mediaFolder` — gallery: save — sub-folder inside Pictures / Movies / Music / Download (Android 10+; no permission needed); `fsGrant` — folders: grant (from fs.pick / fs.grants) — the folder you chose in the system picker; `fsPath` — folders: path inside the folder, a/b/c.txt (list: a folder; empty — the root); `fileName` — save: file name; `fileMime` — save: MIME type (image/jpeg, text/csv…); `fileData` — save / write: the content as base64 (empty — the text input or the «text» field is written; up to 4 MB per call); `fileAppend` — fs.write: append to the end; `fileOffset` — read: from byte (the reply has next and eof — repeat from next until eof); `fileLimit` — read: bytes per call (0 — 256 kB, the most); `fileText` — read: as UTF-8 text in the field text (otherwise base64 in data); `callMode` — call recording: auto — the service records every call by itself (set), off — stop (allow Phone for the app; the law of many countries requires warning the other person); `callSrc` — call recording: source — auto = voice_call (both sides) with root, else the microphone (the other side is heard only on the speaker; new Android may mute the app's microphone during a call). downlink = the other side, uplink = you (root, depends on the phone); `callRate` — call recording: sample rate, Hz; `callMin` — call recording: delete recordings shorter than, s (a rejected / missed call); `recName` — recording: file name prefix (walk-20261031-090503.jsonl); `recSubs` — recording: sources, separated by commas (gps, wifi, ble, bt, cell, accel, gyro, sys…; sms, mic and pkt are never recorded) — it keeps going with no page and after a reboot; `recHz` — recording: sensor rate, Hz (0 — 20); `recWifi` — recording: Wi-Fi scan every, s (0 — 10); `recCell` — recording: cell poll every, s (0 — 5); `recMax` — recording: new file after, MB (0 — 20); the oldest files go over 300 MB in total; `recFile` — recording: file name (from rec.list) for read / export / delete; `recOffset` — recording: read — from byte (the answer has next and eof); `calTitle` — calendar: event title; `calStart` — calendar: start — 2026-11-02 09:30, 2026-11-02, 9:30, tomorrow 9:30, +2h; `calEnd` — calendar: end — the same forms, +1h is from the start (empty — an hour; all day — the end day itself is not included); `calAllDay` — calendar: all-day event; `calWhere` — calendar: place; `calNote` — calendar: note; `calRemind` — calendar: reminders, minutes before the start, up to 5 (10, 60, 1440) — the Calendar app shows them; `calQuery` — calendar: list — only events with this text; `calDays` — calendar: list — days ahead from now; `calId` — calendar: id of the calendar (cal.calendars shows them; empty — the first writable); `calEvent` — calendar: id of the event to remove (from cal.list); `bootPatches` — boot patches: names separated by «;» (boot.set saves the list, boot.run / boot.stop take it or all; they run in the app without a window, up to 6); `alarmEv` — system events → rec: alarm.* (fire, fallback, snooze, dismiss…), boot.* (patches without a window), callrec.* (start, stop, dropped, error) (asks for «phone state» permission); `smsIn` — incoming SMS → rec (only the page of the app); `notifyEv` — taps on the notification buttons → rec; `torchState` — torch state → output torch; `reconnect`

### Batch Check (list → results in the same table)

`batch` · used in 2 presets

- **In:** `rows` (bands), `go` (num), `list` (txt), `result` (rec)
- **Out:** `rec` (rec), `task` (rec), `rows` (bands), `text` (txt), `ok` (num), `bad` (num), `progress` (num), `busy` (num), `new` (num)
- **Parameters:** `url` — bridge: the phone app (ws://phone:8765) or the Node bridge on a computer (ws://127.0.0.1:8777: ping, tcp, http(s), whois; SMS / USSD only on the phone) — an address, or @name from Settings → Devices; `list` — list (storage) — the rows to check (the «rows» wire or the «list» input overrides); `key` — key column — put the same one in the Table (upsert) so a result lands in its row; `target` — column with the address (empty — the first of target, host, ip, url, stream, domain, to, number, phone); `kind` — what to check (a «kind» column in a row overrides it); node — hand every row to your own graph: «task» out → modules → «result» in; `via` — http / iptv / domain: through the bridge (any site, https), or the browser fetch (CORS; the CORS proxy from Settings is used if set), auto — the bridge if it is reachable; `conc` — at the same time (SMS and USSD always go one by one); `timeout` — wait for one check, ms; `every` — repeat every N s after the run ends (0 — only on Run / go); `auto` — run once when the patch starts; `retries` — repeat a failed check this many times (a «retries» column overrides; SMS is never repeated); `retryDelay` — pause between attempts, s (a «retryDelay» column overrides); `nodeTimeout` — node: wait for the answer on «result», s (a «timeoutS» column overrides); `nodeConc` — node: tasks in the handler at the same time (1 — one device; more — the handler must keep the key field in the answer); `cond` — node: success condition, JS over the answer r and the task t, e.g. r.level > t.min (empty — any answer without ok=0 counts); `count` — ping: packets (a «count» column overrides); `port` — tcp: port (a «port» column or host:port overrides); `expect` — expected code: 200, 2xx, 200,301, <400 (an «expect» column overrides); `contains` — the page must contain this text, or /regex/i (a «contains» column overrides); `selector` — the page must have this css block, e.g. #promo (browser parsing; a «selector» column overrides); `minBytes` — a raw stream must give at least this many bytes (a «minbytes» column overrides); `warnDays` — bad when the domain expires in fewer days (a «warn» column overrides); `text` — message (a «text» column overrides); to — the number column (to / number / phone); `deliveryWait` — wait for the delivery report, s (0 — do not wait; carriers may send none); `code` — USSD code (a «code» column overrides); `sim` — SIM slot from 0 (empty — default; a «sim» column overrides); `ussdWait` — wait for the network answer, s; `balanceRe` — regex whose group 1 is the balance (empty — find the number near «баланс / balance / остаток»)

- **In:** `write` (txt)
- **Out:** `value` (num), `hex` (txt), `rec` (rec), `new` (num)
- **Parameters:** `svc` — service: name, 16-bit (180D) or 128-bit UUID; `chr` — characteristic: name, 16-bit (2A37) or 128-bit UUID; `fmt` — value format; `k` — scale; `ofs` — offset; `mode` — get the value; `period` — read period, s; `wfmt` — `write` input is; `ack` — write with response; `name` — device name starts with (empty — by service); `any` — list all devices (ignore the filters); `reconnect`

### BLE GATT / Bluetooth (DSP app)

`gattlink` · used in 7 presets

- **In:** `go` (num), `addr` (txt), `svc` (txt), `chr` (txt), `data` (txt), `cmd` (rec)
- **Out:** `rec` (rec), `value` (num), `hex` (txt), `text` (txt), `ok` (num), `new` (num)
- **Parameters:** `url` — bridge of the DSP app — an address, or @name from Settings → Devices; `action` — action (the Run button, or a rising edge on go); `addr` — device address AA:BB:CC:DD:EE:FF (the «addr» input overrides it); `svc` — service UUID (16- or 128-bit; name from Reference/Bluetooth UUIDs via lookup() in Table); `chr` — characteristic UUID; `data` — write: hex bytes or text (the «data» input overrides it); `wfmt` — write as; `resp` — write with response; `autoRead` — after discover: read every readable characteristic; `autoNotify` — after discover: subscribe to every notifying characteristic; `readMax` — auto-read: at most N characteristics (0 — all); `phy` — PHY; `mtu` — request MTU (0 — don't ask); `prio` — connection priority; `autoConn` — auto-connect (wait for the device instead of a single attempt); `uuid` — RFCOMM service UUID (empty — SPP 1101); `secure` — secure link (RFCOMM / L2CAP, encrypted); `psm` — L2CAP PSM (LE: 128…255; the listener gets its own and reports it); `srvDef` — services: «svc/chr props = value» per line (props: read write writenr notify indicate manual secure; value: hex or "text"). Start advertising with connectable on and the service UUIDs so centrals can find it; `srvReq` — respond: the req number of a «manual» read / write record; `srvStatus` — respond: ATT status (0 — ok with the data, 3 — write not permitted, 257 — failure); `advName` — include the device name; `advUuids` — service UUIDs, comma separated (180d, fe95…); `advMfr` — manufacturer data: id=hex (004C=0215AABBCCDD…), «;» for more; `advSvc` — service data: uuid=hex; `advMode` — advertise mode; `advPower` — tx power; `advConn` — connectable; `advExt` — extended advertising (2M / Coded PHY, >31 bytes); `advTimeout` — stop after, ms (0 — until ble.adv.stop); `advPeriodic` — advertise with a periodic train (needs LE periodic advertising; not connectable). Android apps cannot receive one — use an ESP32 / nRF / another stack to sync to it; `advPint` — periodic interval, ms (100 … 81900); `advPmfr` — periodic data: manufacturer id=hex (FFFF=0102…); «adv pdata» replaces it while running; `advPsvc` — periodic data: service uuid=hex; `fmt` — value format (auto — Reference/Bluetooth UUIDs or hex); `scale`; `reconnect`

- **In:** `rtc` (rtc)
- **Out:** `iq` (iq), `sr` (num)
- **Parameters:** `url` — WebSocket: ws:// wss:// (rtl_tcp: through a bridge, e.g. websockify 8766 host:1234) — or @name from Settings → Devices (the token stays there); `proto` — protocol (rtl_tcp (DSP app): the app bridge or node tools/node/bridge.mjs opens the TCP connection to a standard rtl_tcp server, no websockify; rtl_tcp (WebRTC): through the WebRTC Data connection, the server is the rtl_tcp Server node on the other device; wire its link to rtc); `fmt` — raw stream: sample format (interleaved I, Q); `host` — rtl_tcp (DSP app): server host — 127.0.0.1 is the machine of the bridge itself (the RTL-SDR driver app on the phone), or a Raspberry Pi / another computer: rtl_tcp -a 0.0.0.0; `port` — rtl_tcp (DSP app): server port; `sr` — sample rate, Hz (rtl_tcp: sent to the server; SpyServer: nearest decimation step); `freq` — center frequency, Hz (rtl_tcp, SpyServer: tuned; raw stream: only labels the stream); `gain` — rtl_tcp: tuner gain, dB (0 — auto); `ppm` — rtl_tcp: frequency correction, ppm; `agc` — rtl_tcp: RTL AGC; `bias` — rtl_tcp: bias-T (V4 and similar); `direct` — rtl_tcp: direct sampling branch (HF); `spygain` — SpyServer: gain index (−1 — leave as the server has it); `reconnect`

### Network (DSP app)

`netlink` · used in 10 presets

- **In:** `go` (num), `host` (txt), `data` (txt), `cmd` (rec)
- **Out:** `rec` (rec), `text` (txt), `hex` (txt), `ok` (num), `new` (num)
- **Parameters:** `url` — bridge of the DSP app — an address, or @name from Settings → Devices; `action` — action (the Run button, or a rising edge on go); `host` — host (the «host» input overrides it); `port`; `conn` — connection # (from the «open» / «accept» record); `data` — send: hex or text (the «data» input overrides it); `dfmt` — send as; `lan` — tcp listen: allow other devices on the network; `broadcast` — udp: broadcast; `timeout` — timeout, ms; `count` — pings to send; `size` — payload size, bytes; `ttl` — TTL override (0 — default; «traceroute step» uses this); `dnsType` — record type; `dnsServer` — DNS server (@dns — the network's own); `dnsPort` — port; `dnsEdns` — EDNS (1232-byte UDP, needed for DNSSEC / large answers); `dnsRd` — recursion desired; `nsdType` — service type (discover: what to look for; register: what to announce); `nsdResolve` — discover: resolve each found service (host, port, TXT); `nsdTimeout` — discover: stop after, ms (0 — until «nsd stop»); `nsdName` — register: instance name (the «port» field above is the port); `nsdTxt` — register: TXT records «key=value; key=value» (keys up to 9 characters); `rttBssids` — RTT: access points AA:BB:…, comma separated (the «host» input overrides; empty — the strongest 802.11mc ones from the last Wi-Fi scan, so run a Phone sensors node with Wi-Fi on); `rttCount` — RTT: rounds; `rttEvery` — RTT: pause between rounds, ms (Android limits background apps to a few rounds per 10 minutes); `rttMax` — RTT: at most N access points per round; `awareService` — Aware: service name; `awareSolicited` — publish: solicited (answer only to subscribers' queries); `awareActive` — subscribe: active (ask, not only listen); `awarePeer` — send: peer # (from a «peer» / «message» record); the «data» field is the info for publish / subscribe and the text for send; `monIface` — interface (needs root, iw and «Allow packet capture» in the app Settings; the phone's own Wi-Fi is off while it listens); `monChannel` — channel (0 — not set); `monFreq` — or frequency, MHz (overrides the channel; 6 GHz channels are ambiguous); `monWidth` — width (empty — the driver's default); `monHop` — on: hop between channels, e.g. 1,6,11 (empty — stay on one); `monDwell` — hop: time on each channel, ms; `p2pAddr` — Wi-Fi Direct: peer address from a «peer» record (the «host» input overrides); `p2pIntent` — Wi-Fi Direct: wish to own the group, 0…15 (-1 — let Android decide); `p2pName` — group create: name DIRECT-xy-… (empty both — Android picks name and passphrase; Android 10+ to set them); `p2pPass` — group create: passphrase, 8…63 characters; `wifiSsid` — suggest / join: network name (the «host» input overrides); suggest remove with it empty removes all; `wifiPass` — suggest / join: passphrase (empty — open network); `wifiWpa3` — passphrase is WPA3 (SAE), not WPA2; `wifiBssid` — only this access point, AA:BB:… (empty — any with that name); `wifiHidden` — suggest: the SSID is hidden; `wifiPriority` — suggest: priority (Android 12+; 0 — default); `wifiBind` — join: send the app's own traffic (tcp / udp / ping) over that network, though it has no internet; `reconnect`

They must ALSO be let through the tunnel above (mode/apps), or their traffic never reaches here; `reconnect`

`bstat` — battery electric, every second → «cur» mA (sign depends on the phone, usually − when discharging) «volt» «uah» (charge counted, µAh) (DSP app; where the phone gives no values the app reads /sys through root — «Allow root features»); `statEvery` — battery electric: poll every, s (DSP app; the power policy slows it further); `usage` — app usage today: top apps by time in foreground → «umin» (top app minutes) «utop» (its package) + mAh per app since last charge in rec (DSP app; enable «Usage access» in the app Settings; mAh needs DUMP via adb «pm grant app.dsp.phone android.permission.DUMP» or root); `accel` — accelerometer; `gyro` — gyroscope; `mag` — magnetometer; `light`; `pressure`; `gravity` — gravity (DSP app) → rec; `linear` — linear acceleration, no gravity (DSP app) → rec; `rotation` — rotation vector → yaw / pitch / roll (DSP app); `gamerot` — game rotation vector, no magnetometer (DSP app) → rec; `georot` — geomagnetic rotation vector (DSP app) → rec; `accelraw` — accelerometer, uncalibrated + bias (DSP app) → rec; `gyroraw` — gyroscope, uncalibrated + drift bias (DSP app) → rec; `magraw` — magnetometer, uncalibrated + bias (DSP app) → rec; `proximity` — proximity, cm (DSP app); `ambtemp` — ambient temperature, °C (DSP app; few phones have it); `humidity` — relative humidity, % (DSP app; few phones have it); `steps` — step counter since boot (DSP app; needs Physical activity permission); `heading` — compass heading, ° (DSP app; Android 14+); `hinge` — hinge angle, ° (DSP app; foldables); `wifi` — Wi-Fi scan (with the position; DSP app: channel, width, security, standard, WPS, country); `wifiEvery` — Wi-Fi scan every, s (DSP app; Android limits it); `ble` — Bluetooth LE advertisements (DSP app); `bt` — Bluetooth classic discovery (DSP app); `gnss` — GNSS: every satellite — system, C/N0, elevation, azimuth, used in fix (DSP app); `gnssraw` — GNSS: raw measurements — pseudorange rate, carrier phase, clock (DSP app); `nmea` — NMEA sentences → output «nmeatxt» → NMEA Parser (DSP app); `devEvery` — Wi-Fi / BLE / BT / cells: one record per device at most every N s (0 — all); `devMove` — … and only after moving N m (0 — any); `cell` — cell towers: operator, cell id, band, RSRP / RSRQ / SINR (rec; serving cell → cdbm crsrp crsrq csinr); `cellEvery` — cell poll every, s (DSP app); `micOn` — microphone → output «mic» (signal), level in «micdb» (DSP app, also with the screen locked); `micHz` — microphone: sample rate, Hz (the DSP app tries the nearest standard one if the phone cannot open it; the real one is in the record, sr). Above 8 kHz of signal needs 22050 and more; `micSrc` — microphone: source in the DSP app — auto = unprocessed (no noise suppression / AGC) if the phone has it, else mic; voice_call / downlink (the other side) / uplink (your voice) / submix (what the phone plays) need root («Allow root features» in the app Settings) and the phone's audio HAL to give them; `micAddr` — microphone: input of the phone (DSP app) — the list is filled from what the phone reports while the microphone runs (open it after connecting; «bottom», «back»…). auto — by «preferred input» only; `micCh` — microphone: channels (DSP app) — stereo = two microphones of the phone in one recording: «mic» is the left, «micB» the right channel, samples of both from the same ADC frame, so there is no time shift between them (the phase difference is real). Use source unprocessed or camcorder; the order of the microphones depends on the phone; if it has no stereo input the app falls back to mono (see the status); `micLow` — microphone: low latency (DSP app) — pieces of 10 ms, the bridge sends them at once, a small buffer on the phone and a short smoothing reserve here (below). Costs more CPU and risks gaps on a busy phone; for the least delay also set Block size 128 in the toolbar; `micLag` — microphone: smoothing reserve in low latency, ms (less — shorter delay, but gaps if the pieces come unevenly); `micDev` — microphone: preferred input in the DSP app (a USB microphone / headset, wired, Bluetooth, built-in); noise suppression, AGC and echo cancellation are switched off anyway; `ext` — events from other apps (Tasker / MacroDroid intent app.dsp.phone.EXT) → rec, number in «extv» (DSP app; allow in its Settings); `pkt` — packet capture, frames in hex → rec (DSP app; root + tcpdump; allow it in the app Settings); `iface` — capture: interface (any, wlan0, rmnet_data0…); `filter` — capture: filter (tcpdump syntax, e.g. tcp port 80); `snap` — capture: bytes per frame; `activity` — activity: still / moving / walking + screen locked → «act» (0 still, 1 moving, 2 walking) «cadence» «locked» «screen»; low power, works with the screen locked (DSP app; log to a file in its Settings); `pickup` — pick-up gesture (phone lifted) → counter «pick»; `wakegest` — wake gesture → counter «wakeg»; `glance` — glance gesture → counter «glanc»; `tilt` — tilt detector → counter «tiltc»; `offbody` — off-body detector (watches, bands) → «offb» 1 on the body, 0 taken off; `step` — step detector (DSP app) → rec; `devorient` — screen orientation 0–3 (DSP app; Android 13+); `sigmotion` — significant motion (DSP app; one-shot trigger); `stationary` — stationary detect → still=1 (DSP app); `motion` — motion detect → still=0 (DSP app); `hz` — sensor rate, Hz (DSP app; 0 — default; the phone gives what the sensor can); `direct` — direct sensor channel (DSP app, Android 8+): the sensor writes into shared memory — steady 50 / 200 / 800 Hz without jitter, for vibration analysis (rate above → level; a sensor without it keeps the normal way); `reconnect`

### Video Player

`vplayer` · used in 7 presets

- **In:** `url` (txt), `rows` (bands), `channel` (val), `seek` (val), `next` (num)
- **Out:** `vid` (vid), `rec` (rec), `name` (txt), `time` (num), `dur` (num), `pct` (num), `playing` (num), `end` (num)
- **Parameters:** `url` — URL: a video file (mp4 / webm) or an HLS playlist (.m3u8); a list of sources comes on «rows»; `speed`; `engine` — who plays (always this player’s own <video>): auto — the browser, and in the DSP app libVLC repacks into a stream what the browser cannot open (smb:// ftp:// sftp:// nfs:// upnp:// rtmp:// udp:// rtp:// rtsp://, mkv / ts / avi) and what it failed to play; browser — never libVLC; vlc stream — any address through libVLC; `smode` — libVLC stream: auto — H.264 and AAC / MP3 pass as they are (cheap), the rest (DivX, MPEG-2, VP9, AC3, DTS…) is re-encoded to H.264 / AAC; remux — never re-encode; transcode — always; `skbps` — libVLC stream: video bitrate when re-encoding, kbit/s; `swidth` — libVLC stream: width when re-encoding, px (0 — as in the source); `sdeint` — libVLC stream: deinterlace (re-encodes the video); `sburn` — libVLC stream: burn the chosen subtitles into the picture (re-encodes the video); `sadelay` — libVLC stream: audio delay, ms (applies on the next open / seek / track change); `scaching` — libVLC stream: network cache, ms; `bridge` — bridge of the DSP app, for torrents (magnet: links, .torrent files) and for the libVLC stream: an address, or @name from Settings → Devices (the token stays there); from another computer — the phone (needs «share on LAN», the token and «open commands» on, and a page on http://: an https:// page blocks http:// streams of a LAN address); `tfile` — open a .torrent file (trackers with a login do not give it by a link); `capture` — allow picture capture for the floating window / PiP of the app (the server must send CORS headers — otherwise the video will not play); without it the window shows a note instead of the picture; `autoplay` — start playing at once (the browser may require a click first); `advance` — at the end go on to the next source of the list

### VLC Source

`vlcsrc` · used in 2 presets

- **In:** `url` (txt), `go` (num), `pause` (num)
- **Out:** `l` (sig), `r` (sig), `title` (txt), `pos` (num), `dur` (num), `playing` (num), `end` (num), `video` (txt)
- **Parameters:** `url` — address: a file or a stream on the network (http / https / HLS), multicast udp://@239.1.1.1:1234 or rtp://@…, rtsp://, rtmp://, smb://, ftp://, sftp://, nfs://, upnp:// — any format libVLC knows (AC3 / DTS / FLAC / AAC / Opus …); `rate` — sample rate asked from libVLC (graph — the rate of the graph); the signal is interpolated to the graph rate anyway; `channels` — channels: stereo → «l» and «r»; mono → both outputs the same; `caching` — libVLC network cache, ms (more — fewer dropouts on a bad network, a later start); `lag` — smoothing reserve in the graph, ms (less — shorter delay, but gaps if the pieces come unevenly); `video` — picture: libVLC takes the picture of the address (a second connection to it) and serves it as MJPEG on 127.0.0.1; the address comes out on the «video» pin — wire it to the «url» of IP Camera; `vfps` — picture: frames per second; `vwidth` — picture: width, px; `vkbps` — picture: quality (bitrate of MJPEG), kbit/s; `bridge` — bridge of the DSP app: ws://127.0.0.1:8765 or a device name (@phone)

- **Parameters:** `ice` — STUN (stun:stun.l.google.com:19302), empty — same network only; `remote` — remote code (paste the other side's offer / answer); `sigurl` — auto-connect: MQTT broker WebSocket (wss://…) — exchanges the codes for you; `room` — auto-connect: room (a long secret name, the same on both sides; it encrypts the signalling); `sigUser` — broker user (optional); `sigPass` — broker password (saved with the patch!); `auto` — auto-connect and reconnect; `mic` — microphone (voice); `mute` — mute my microphone; `camera`; `facing` — camera: front / rear; `screen` — share screen; `speaker` — play the other side (sound and picture); `maxMB` — refuse incoming files over, MB

## Tools

### Agent (LLM with tools)

`agent` · used in 2 presets

- **Parameters:** `task` — task (the wire «task» starts a run by itself when its text changes); `tools` — what the model sees and can do — every line makes a pin: read name[:type] "meaning" · write name[:type] [min..max] "meaning" · act name(arg[:type] [min..max], …) [-> result_pin] "meaning" [confirm|auto]; `allow` — built-in tools allowed: calc, time, read, write, http_get, notify, speak (notify / speak — in the DSP app); the actions you declare are always there; `hosts` — http_get: allowed hosts, https only (e.g. api.open-meteo.com); empty — the tool does not work; `maxSteps` — most steps (model answers) per task; `timeout` — wait for the model, s (then the run stops and the model is told to halt); `confirmWait` — wait for your approval of an action, s (then it is denied); `actWait` — an action with «-> pin»: wait for the answer on that pin, s
- The pins depend on the module settings.

Host «app»: a list key (qwen2.5-3b-instruct-q4_0), a .gguf file on the phone or an address of a .gguf — downloaded once; `appGpu` — host «app»: use the GPU (OpenCL, Adreno 7xx / 8xx; fastest with Q4_0 models); off — CPU only. Switching CPU → GPU needs an app restart; `maxTok` — host «app»: longest answer, tokens; `threads` — host «app»: threads (0 — automatic: up to 4 fast cores (generation is memory-bound, more threads slow it down); «⏱ Benchmark» picks the best on this phone); `prompt` — question (the wire «prompt» overrides it); `sys` — system prompt (the wire «system» overrides it); `auto` — ask by itself when the «prompt» wire changes; `keep` — exchanges kept in the dialogue (0 — every question alone)

### Nipkow Disk: Receive

`nipkowRx` · used in 4 presets

- **In:** `level` (sig), `sync` (sig), `trim` (num), `shift` (num)
- **Out:** `img` (img), `lock` (num), `rpm` (num)
- **Parameters:** `std` — disk: a ready-made variant (fills in the fields below; then edit freely); `lines` — lines per field = holes on one spiral; `spirals` — spirals = fields per turn of the disk; `interlace` — interlace: spirals are shifted by 1/k of the line pitch, a frame is k fields (otherwise every field is a whole picture); `color` — colour: red, green and blue pictures go in turn (a filter wheel), one colour frame = 3 pictures (3 frames when interlaced); the start of the cycle is marked by the sync; `fps` — pictures per second, each colour plane counts (turns per second = fps ÷ spirals; ×60 = rpm for one spiral); `px` — pixels per line (auto: about 4 samples per pixel; both ends must agree); `aspect` — window width : height — sets the inner radius of the spiral; `sweep` — the first line of a field is at the outer or inner edge of the spiral; `rot` — direction of rotation (mirrors the picture in the disk geometry); `sync` — sync: pulse — separate signal once per turn; in-band — an extra hole before every field, below black; none/free/auto — no sync; `view` — picture: flat — straight lines; disk — as seen through the disk: lines are arcs of the spiral (a sector); `size` — output picture width, px (height follows the window shape); `invert` — negative; `auto` — auto level: stretch the picture to the full range; `trim` — free / auto start: turn period trim, ppm (the picture stops sliding when it matches the transmitter); `shift` — picture shift along the field, fraction (needed in free and auto: no sync marks where the field begins)

### Nipkow Disk: Transmit

`nipkowTx` · used in 3 presets

- **In:** `img` (img), `vid` (vid)
- **Out:** `level` (sig), `sync` (sig), `rpm` (num)
- **Parameters:** `std` — disk: a ready-made variant (fills in the fields below; then edit freely); `lines` — lines per field = holes on one spiral; `spirals` — spirals = fields per turn of the disk; `interlace` — interlace: spirals are shifted by 1/k of the line pitch, a frame is k fields (otherwise every field is a whole picture); `color` — colour: red, green and blue pictures go in turn (a filter wheel), one colour frame = 3 pictures (3 frames when interlaced); the start of the cycle is marked by the sync; `fps` — pictures per second, each colour plane counts (turns per second = fps ÷ spirals; ×60 = rpm for one spiral); `px` — pixels per line (auto: about 4 samples per pixel; both ends must agree); `aspect` — window width : height — sets the inner radius of the spiral; `sweep` — the first line of a field is at the outer or inner edge of the spiral; `rot` — direction of rotation (mirrors the picture in the disk geometry); `sync` — sync: pulse — separate signal once per turn; in-band — an extra hole before every field, below black; none/free/auto — no sync; `scene` — how the picture is read: flat — lines are straight; disk — along the arcs the holes really draw (the picture comes out bent); `src` — picture: auto — input if connected, otherwise the test pattern; `fit` — picture to the window: crop to its shape, or squeeze; `invert` — negative (white = low level); `amp` — output level
